# Real Madrid CF

"Real Madrid" redirects here. For the basketball team, see [Real Madrid Baloncesto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid_Baloncesto). For other uses related to the club, see [Real Madrid (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid_\(disambiguation\)).

"Los Blancos" redirects here. For the Argentine village, see [Los Blancos, Salta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Blancos,_Salta).

**Real Madrid Club de Fútbol** (Spanish pronunciation: [\[reˈal maˈðɾið ˈkluβ ðe ˈfuðβol\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Spanish) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/4/43/Real_Madrid.ogg/Real_Madrid.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/4/43/Real_Madrid.ogg/Real_Madrid.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>) is a Spanish professional [association football club](10568.md) based in [Madrid](41188263.md). The club competes in [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga), the top tier of [Spanish football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_football_league_system).

Founded in 1902 as Madrid Football Club, the club has traditionally worn a white home kit. The [honorific title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Title_of_honour) 'Real' is Spanish for "Royal" and was bestowed by [Alfonso XIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfonso_XIII) in 1920. Real Madrid have played their home matches in the [Bernabéu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernab%C3%A9u_\(stadium\)) since 1947. Unlike most European sporting clubs, Real Madrid's members have owned and operated the club throughout its history. The club is one of the most widely supported in the world and the most followed sports club across social media.[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10] It was estimated to be worth \$6.6 billion in 2024, making it the world's [most valuable football club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes_list_of_the_most_valuable_football_clubs).[^cite_note-Forbes2024-11] In 2024, it became the first football club to make €1 billion (\$1.08bn) in revenue.[^cite_note-12] The club anthem is the "[Hala Madrid y nada más](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hala_Madrid_y_nada_m%C3%A1s)".[^cite_note-13]

Real Madrid is one of the [most successful football clubs in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_football_clubs_by_competitive_honours_won) and [the most decorated club in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UEFA_club_competition_winners). In [Spanish domestic football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_records_and_statistics_in_Spain), the club has won 71 trophies; [a record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga_records_and_statistics#Titles) 36 La Liga titles, 20 [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey), 13 [Supercopa de España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a), a [Copa Eva Duarte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Eva_Duarte) and a [Copa de la Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_de_la_Liga).[^cite_note-14] [In International football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid_CF_in_international_football), Real Madrid have won [a record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UEFA_club_competition_winners#Winners) 35 trophies: [a record](44220.md#Performance_by_club) 15 [European Cup/UEFA Champions League](44220.md) titles, [a record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_UEFA_Super_Cup_finals#Performances) six [UEFA Super Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Super_Cup), two [UEFA Cups](232175.md), a joint-record two [Latin Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Cup), a record one [Iberoamerican Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Iberoamericana), and a record nine [world championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_world_champion_football_clubs#Winners).[^cite_note-Club_World_Cup-18] Madrid has been ranked joint first a record number of times in the [IFFHS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IFFHS) Club World Ranking.[^cite_note-19] In [UEFA](32332.md)'s club ranking, Madrid ranks first in the [all-time club ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Cup_and_UEFA_Champions_League_records_and_statistics#Overall_team_records).[^cite_note-uefa_records22-20][^cite_note-21]

As one of the three founding members of [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) [never relegated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_unrelegated_association_football_clubs) from the top division along with [Barcelona](68187.md) and [Athletic Bilbao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletic_Bilbao), Real Madrid has many long-standing rivalries, most notably *[El Clásico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Cl%C3%A1sico)* with Barcelona and *[El Derbi Madrileño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Derby)* with [Atlético Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Madrid).[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] The club established itself as a major force in Spanish and European football during the 1950s and 60s, winning five consecutive and six overall [European Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Champion_Clubs'_Cup). This success was replicated on the domestic front, with Madrid winning 12 league titles in 16 years. This team, which included [Alfredo Di Stéfano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Di_St%C3%A9fano), [Ferenc Puskás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferenc_Pusk%C3%A1s), [Paco Gento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paco_Gento) and [Raymond Kopa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_Kopa), is considered by some in the sport to be the greatest of all time.[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] Real Madrid is known for its *[Galácticos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gal%C3%A1cticos)* policy, which involves signing the world's best players, such as [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)), [Zinedine Zidane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinedine_Zidane) and [David Beckham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Beckham) to create a superstar team.[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-tft-28] In 2009, Madrid signed [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md) for a record-breaking £80 million (€94 million) from [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.);[^cite_note-29] he became the club's and history's all-time top goal-scorer.[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33] In addition to signing star players, Real Madrid develops homegrown talent through its academy, *[La Fábrica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_F%C3%A1brica_\(Real_Madrid\))*, which has produced notable graduates such as [Raúl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C3%BAl_\(footballer\)), [Iker Casillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iker_Casillas), and [Dani Carvajal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Carvajal), and has supplied the highest number of players to Europe's [top five leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Five_\(association_football\)).[^cite_note-34]

Real Madrid was recognized as the [greatest football club of the 20th century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Club_of_the_Century),[^cite_note-35] receiving the [FIFA Centennial Order of Merit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Order_of_Merit) in 2004.[^cite_note-36] Real Madrid has the highest number of participations in the European Cup/UEFA Champions League (55),[^cite_note-uefa_records22-20] a tournament in which they hold the records for most wins, draws and goals scored.[^cite_note-37] Real Madrid is the only club to have won three consecutive titles in the European Cup/UEFA Champions League twice, achieving this in [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1955%E2%80%9356_European_Cup)-[58](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1957%E2%80%9358_European_Cup) and [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_UEFA_Champions_League)-[18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_UEFA_Champions_League).[^cite_note-38] In 2024, they won a record-extending [15th Champions League title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_UEFA_Champions_League) (the sixth in eleven seasons), recognized as such by [Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records).[^cite_note-39] Real Madrid is the first club across all [Europe's top-five leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Five_\(association_football\)) to win [100 trophies in all competitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_football_clubs_in_Spain_by_major_honours_won).[^cite_note-40] As of May 2026, Real Madrid are ranked 2nd in [UEFA club rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_coefficient#Men's_Club_coefficient), and first over 2013–23 coefficient.[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42]

## History

Main article: [History of Real Madrid CF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Real_Madrid_CF)

See also: [List of Real Madrid CF seasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Real_Madrid_CF_seasons)

### Early years (1902–1943)



[Julián Palacios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juli%C3%A1n_Palacios), the first president of the club in 1900–1902

Real Madrid's origins go back to when football was introduced to Madrid by the academics and students of the *[Institución Libre de Enseñanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instituci%C3%B3n_Libre_de_Ense%C3%B1anza)*, which included several [Cambridge](25978572.md) and [Oxford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxford) University graduates.[^cite_note-43] They founded *(Sociedad) Sky Football* in 1897, commonly known as *La Sociedad* (The Society) as it was the only one based in Madrid, playing on Sunday mornings at Moncloa.

In 1900, conflict between members caused some of them to leave and create a new club, *Nueva Sociedad de Football* (New Society of Football), to distinguish themselves from *Sky Football*. Among the dissenters were [Julián Palacios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juli%C3%A1n_Palacios), recognized as the first Real Madrid president, [Juan Padrós](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Padr%C3%B3s) and [Carlos Padrós](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Padr%C3%B3s), the latter two being brothers and future presidents of Real Madrid. In 1901, this new club was renamed as Madrid Football Club. Later, following a restructuring in 1902, *Sky* was renamed as "[New Foot-Ball Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Foot-Ball_Club)".[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45][^cite_note-Ball,_Phil_p._117-46]



Madrid FC team in 1902

On 6 March 1902, after a new board presided by Juan Padrós had been elected, Madrid Football Club was officially founded.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(I)-7] The Padrós brothers summoned other football enthusiasts to a meeting in the back room of *Al Capricho*, the family business. They viewed football as a mass sport that should be accessible to representatives of all social classes, and thought the new club should embody that idea. The brothers proposed the name, Madrid Football Club, which was unanimously accepted. The membership fee was also set, two [pesetas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_peseta) a month, and the color of the shirt was chosen to be white in honour of a famous English team [Corinthian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corinthian_F.C.), which Juan Padrós had met on one of his trips.[^cite_note-sapiens.cat-47][^cite_note-48]



Madrid FC team in 1906

Three years after its founding, in [1905](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1905_Copa_del_Rey), *Madrid FC* won its first title after defeating [Athletic Bilbao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletic_Bilbao) in the [Spanish Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey) final. The club became one of the founding sides of the [Royal Spanish Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Spanish_Football_Federation) on 4 January 1909, when club president [Adolfo Meléndez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolfo_Mel%C3%A9ndez) signed the foundation agreement of the Spanish FA. After moving between several grounds, the team relocated to the *[Campo de O'Donnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campo_de_O'Donnell)* in 1912.[^cite_note-History_—_Chapter_1_-_From_the_Estrada_Lot_to_the_nice,_little_O'Donnel_pitch-49] In 1920, the club's name was changed to Real Madrid after [King Alfonso XIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfonso_XIII) granted the title of Real (Royal) to the club.[^cite_note-50]

In [1929](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1929_La_Liga), the first [Spanish football league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) was founded. Real Madrid led the first league season until the last match, a loss to Athletic Bilbao, meant they finished runners-up to Barcelona.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(III)-51] Real Madrid won its first league title in the [1931–32 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1931%E2%80%9332_La_Liga) and retained it [the following year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932%E2%80%9333_La_Liga).[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(IV)-52]

On 14 April 1931, the arrival of the [Second Spanish Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Spanish_Republic) caused the club to lose the title Real and the royal crown on its emblem, going back to being named Madrid Football Club until the end of the [Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War). Football continued during the Second World War, and on 13 June 1943, [Madrid beat Barcelona 11–1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid_v_FC_Barcelona_\(1943_Copa_del_General%C3%ADsimo\)) in the second leg of the [Copa del Generalísimo semi-finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1943_Copa_del_General%C3%ADsimo#Semi-finals), the Spanish Cup having been renamed in honour of [General Franco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Franco).[^cite_note-CdR-53][^cite_note-54]

The first leg, played at the [Les Corts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_de_Les_Corts) in Catalonia, had ended with [Barcelona](68187.md) winning 3–0. Madrid complained about all the three goals that referee Fombona Fernández had allowed for Barcelona,[^cite_note-p67-55] with the home supporters also whistling Madrid throughout, whom they accused of employing roughhouse tactics, and Fombona for allowing them to. The newspaper *[Ya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ya_\(newspaper\))* reported the whistling as a "clear intention to attack the representatives of Spain".[^cite_note-56] Barcelona fans were banned from traveling to Madrid. The day of the second leg, the Barcelona team were insulted and stones were thrown at their bus as soon as they left their hotel.

Barcelona's striker [Mariano Gonzalvo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariano_Gonzalvo) said of the incident, "Five minutes before the game had started, our penalty area was already full of coins." Barcelona goalkeeper [Luis Miró](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Mir%C3%B3) rarely approached his line—when he did, he was armed with stones. As [Francisco Calvet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Calvet) told the story: "They were shouting: *Reds! Separatists!*... a bottle just missed Sospedra that would have killed him if it had hit him. It was all set up."[^cite_note-57]

Real Madrid went 2–0 up within half an hour. The third goal brought with it a sending off for Barcelona's Benito García after he made what Calvet claimed was a "completely normal tackle". Madrid's [José Llopis Corona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Llopis_Corona) recalled: "At which point, they got a bit demoralized", while Ángel Mur countered, "at which point, we thought: 'go on then, score as many as you want'".[^cite_note-58] Madrid made it 8–0 by half-time; two goals were also ruled out for offside, and proceeded to score a further three goals in the second half, to which Barcelona replied with a late consolation goal.[^cite_note-Spaaij_2006-59]

According to football writer [Sid Lowe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sid_Lowe): "There have been relatively few mentions of the game \[since] and it is not a result that has been particularly celebrated in Madrid. Indeed, the 11–1 occupies a far more prominent place in Barcelona's history. This was the game that first formed the identification of Madrid as the team of the dictatorship and Barcelona as its victims."[^cite_note-p67-55] Fernando Argila, Barcelona's reserve goalkeeper from the 1943 match, said: "There was no rivalry. Not, at least, until that game."[^cite_note-60]

### Santiago Bernabéu and unprecedented success (1943–1978)



[Alfredo Di Stéfano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Di_St%C3%A9fano) led the club to win five consecutive European Cups (currently the Champions League).

[Santiago Bernabéu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Bernab%C3%A9u_\(footballer\)) became president of Real Madrid in 1943.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(V)-61] Under his presidency, the club was rebuilt after the Civil War, and he oversaw the construction of the club's current stadium, [Estadio Real Madrid Club de Fútbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Bernab%C3%A9u_Stadium) (now known as the Santiago Bernabéu), and its training facilities [Ciudad Deportiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciudad_Deportiva,_Madrid). Additionally, during the 1950s former *Real Madrid Amateurs* player Miguel Malbo founded Real Madrid's youth academy, or "*[cantera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantera)*", known today as [La Fábrica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_F%C3%A1brica_\(Real_Madrid\)). Beginning in 1953, he embarked upon a strategy of signing world-class players from abroad, the most prominent being [Alfredo Di Stéfano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Di_St%C3%A9fano).[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(VI)-62]



[Amancio Amaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amancio_Amaro), captain of the [Yé-yé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C3%A9-y%C3%A9_\(Real_Madrid\)) team of the 1960s

In 1955, acting upon the idea proposed by [Gabriel Hanot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Hanot), a French sports journalist and editor of *[L'Équipe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'%C3%89quipe)*, Bernabéu, Ernest Bedrignan (deputy chairman of the [Ligue de Football Professionnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligue_de_Football_Professionnel))[^cite_note-63] and [Gusztáv Sebes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guszt%C3%A1v_Sebes) created the [European Cup](44220.md), a continental tournament for the league champions around Europe, which is today known as the UEFA Champions League.[^cite_note-64] It was under Bernabéu's guidance that Real Madrid established itself as a major force in both Spanish and European football. The club won the European Cup five times in a row between 1956 and 1960, which included the 7–3 Hampden Park [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960_European_Cup_Final) against [Eintracht Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eintracht_Frankfurt) in [1960](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1959%E2%80%9360_European_Cup).[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(VI)-62] After these five consecutive successes, Real was permanently awarded the original cup and earned the right to wear the [UEFA badge of honour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Champion_Clubs'_Cup#Multiple-winner_badge).[^cite_note-badge_of_honour-65] Real Madrid's achievements in Europe were built upon its unprecedented domestic dominance, with the club winning twelve league titles out of sixteen possible from [1953–54](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953%E2%80%9354_Real_Madrid_CF_season) to [1968–69](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968%E2%80%9369_Real_Madrid_CF_season), including a five-in-a-row sequence in 1961–65, and finishing runners-up a further three times.[^cite_note-66]



Real Madrid's captain [Paco Gento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paco_Gento) shaking hands with [Zamalek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zamalek_SC)'s captain [Sharif El-Far](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharif_El-Far) before their friendly match on the occasion for celebrating 50 years on Zamalek's establishment in [Cairo Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo_International_Stadium) on 10 March 1961

The club won the European Cup for a sixth time in [1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965%E2%80%9366_European_Cup), defeating [Partizan Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FK_Partizan) 2–1 in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_European_Cup_Final) with a team composed entirely of same nationality players, a first in the competition.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(VII)-67] This team became known as the [Yé-yé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C3%A9-y%C3%A9_\(Real_Madrid\)). The name "Yé-yé" came from the "Yeah, yeah, yeah" chorus in [The Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles)' song "[She Loves You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/She_Loves_You)" after four members of the team posed for *[Marca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marca_\(newspaper\))* and impersonated the Beatles.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(VII)-67] The Yé-yé generation was also European Cup runners-up in [1962](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_European_Cup_Final)[^cite_note-Madrid62-68] and [1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_European_Cup_Final).[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(VII)-67] In the 1970s, Real Madrid won six league championships and three Spanish Cups.[^cite_note-69] The club competed in its first [European Cup Winners' Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Cup_Winners'_Cup) in [1970–71](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970%E2%80%9371_European_Cup_Winners'_Cup) and progressed all the way to the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1971_European_Cup_Winners'_Cup_Final), where it lost to English side [Chelsea](7473.md) 2–1 in a replay.[^cite_note-70] On 2 July 1978, club president Santiago Bernabéu died while the [World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup) was being played in [Argentina](18951905.md). [FIFA](11049.md) decreed three days of mourning to honour him during the tournament.[^cite_note-71] The following year, the club organized the first edition of the [Trofeo Santiago Bernabéu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trofeo_Santiago_Bernab%C3%A9u) in memory of its former president.

Bernabéu had been Real Madrid's president for almost 35 years, during which his club won one [Intercontinental Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercontinental_Cup_\(1960%E2%80%932004\)), six European Cups, 16 [league titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Spanish_football_champions), six [Spanish Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_del_Rey), two [Latin Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Cup) and one [Copa Eva Duarte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Eva_Duarte).[^cite_note-BernabeuHistory-72]

### Quinta del Buitre and sustained success (1980–2000)



['*Casillas*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iker_Casillas)'

['*Helguera*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Helguera)'

['*Karanka*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aitor_Karanka)'

['*Campo*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Campo)'

['*Salgado*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%ADchel_Salgado)'

**[Roberto Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Carlos)**

['*Morientes*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Morientes)'

In the early 1980s, Real Madrid had lost its grasp on the La Liga title, until a new cohort of home-grown stars brought [domestic](957951.md) success back to the club.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(IX)-73][^cite_note-marcaquinta-74] Spanish sports journalist Julio César Iglesias gave to this generation the name *[La Quinta del Buitre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinta_del_Buitre)* ("Vulture's Cohort"), which was derived from the nickname given to one of its members, [Emilio Butragueño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilio_Butrague%C3%B1o). The other four members were [Manolo Sanchís](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manolo_Sanch%C3%ADs), [Martín Vázquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Mart%C3%ADn_V%C3%A1zquez), [Míchel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%ADchel_\(footballer,_born_1963\)) and [Miguel Pardeza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Pardeza). All five footballers were graduates of [Real Madrid's youth academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_F%C3%A1brica_\(Real_Madrid\)).[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(IX)-73][^cite_note-marcaquinta-74] With *La Quinta del Buitre* (reduced to four members when Pardeza left for [Zaragoza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Zaragoza) in 1986) and notable players like goalkeeper [Francisco Buyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Buyo), right-back Miguel Porlán *[Chendo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chendo)* and Mexican striker [Hugo Sánchez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_S%C3%A1nchez), Real Madrid had one of the best teams in Spain and Europe during the second half of the 1980s, winning two [UEFA Cups](232175.md), five Spanish championships in a row, one Spanish Cup, and three [Spanish Super Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a).[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(IX)-73][^cite_note-marcaquinta-74] In the early 1990s, *La Quinta del Buitre* split up after Martín Vázquez, Emilio Butragueño and Míchel left the club.

In 1996, President [Lorenzo Sanz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Sanz) appointed [Fabio Capello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Capello) as coach.[^cite_note-75] Although his tenure lasted only one season, Real Madrid were proclaimed league champions, and players like [Predrag Mijatović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predrag_Mijatovi%C4%87), [Davor Šuker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davor_%C5%A0uker), [Clarence Seedorf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_Seedorf), [Roberto Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Carlos) and keeper [Bodo Illgner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodo_Illgner) arrived at the club to strengthen a squad that already boasted the likes of [Raúl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C3%BAl_\(footballer\)), [Fernando Hierro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Hierro) and [Fernando Redondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Redondo). As a result, Real Madrid (with the addition of [Fernando Morientes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Morientes) in 1997) finally ended its 32-year wait for its seventh European Cup: in 1998, under manager [Jupp Heynckes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jupp_Heynckes), they defeated Juventus 1–0 in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_UEFA_Champions_League_Final) with a goal from Mijatović.[^cite_note-76]

In November 1999, [Vicente del Bosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_del_Bosque) took over as coach. For the last season of the century, [1999–2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999%E2%80%932000_Real_Madrid_CF_season), the squad was still led by the older veterans such as Fernando Hierro, Fernando Redondo, Roberto Carlos and Raúl. Real added the budding young talents of [Guti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guti_\(Spanish_footballer\)) and [Iker Casillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iker_Casillas), supported by the arrival of [Steve McManaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_McManaman) and [Nicolas Anelka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Anelka) from the English Premier League, alongside local talents [Míchel Salgado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%ADchel_Salgado) and [Iván Helguera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Helguera). In Del Bosque's first season in charge, Real won the Champions League for the [eighth time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999%E2%80%932000_UEFA_Champions_League), following a 3–0 victory over [Valencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valencia_CF) in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_UEFA_Champions_League_Final), with goals from Morientes, McManaman and Raúl.[^cite_note-77] This victory marked the beginning of a successful period in Real Madrid's history.[^cite_note-bbc_stats-78]

### Florentino Pérez era (2000–2006)

See also: [Galácticos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gal%C3%A1cticos)



**[César](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A9sar_S%C3%A1nchez)**

**[Hierro (c)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Hierro)**

**[Helguera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Helguera)**

**[Salgado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%ADchel_Salgado)**

**[Roberto Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Carlos)**

**[Makélélé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claude_Mak%C3%A9l%C3%A9l%C3%A9)**

**[Figo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lu%C3%ADs_Figo)**

**[Solari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Solari)**

**[Zidane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinedine_Zidane)**

**[Raúl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C3%BAl_\(footballer\))**

**[Morientes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Morientes)**

In July 2000, [Florentino Pérez](2919782.md) was elected club president.[^cite_note-79] He vowed in his campaign to erase the club's €270 million debt and modernize the club's facilities. However, the primary electoral promise that propelled Pérez to victory was the signing of [Luís Figo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lu%C3%ADs_Figo) from arch-rivals Barcelona.[^cite_note-80] The following year, the club had its training ground rezoned and used the money to begin assembling the *[Galácticos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gal%C3%A1cticos)* team by signing a global star every summer, which included [Zinedine Zidane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinedine_Zidane), [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)), [Luís Figo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lu%C3%ADs_Figo), [David Beckham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Beckham) and [Fabio Cannavaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Cannavaro).[^cite_note-2001-2010-81] It is debatable whether the gamble paid off, as despite winning the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001%E2%80%9302_UEFA_Champions_League) and an [Intercontinental Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercontinental_Cup_\(1960%E2%80%932004\)) in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Intercontinental_Cup), followed by [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_La_Liga) in 2003, the club failed to win a major trophy for the next three seasons. Off the pitch, the *Zidanes y Pavones* policy resulted in increased financial success based on the exploitation of the club's high marketing potential around the world, particularly in Asia.[^cite_note-2001-2010-81]



[Beckham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Beckham) and [Zidane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinedine_Zidane) were considered *[Galácticos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gal%C3%A1cticos)*.

The few days after the capturing of the [2003 La Liga title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_La_Liga) were surrounded with controversy. The first controversial decision came when Pérez sacked winning coach [Vicente del Bosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_del_Bosque).[^cite_note-82] Over a dozen players left the club, including Madrid captain Fernando Hierro, while defensive midfielder [Claude Makélélé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claude_Mak%C3%A9l%C3%A9l%C3%A9) refused to take part in training in protest at being one of the lowest-paid players at the club and subsequently moved to [Chelsea](7473.md).[^cite_note-83] "That's a lot \[of players leaving] when the normal rule is: never change a winning team", stated Zidane.[^cite_note-Lowe-84] Real Madrid, with newly appointed coach [Carlos Queiroz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Queiroz), started their domestic league slowly after a hard win over [Real Betis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Betis).[^cite_note-Lowe-84]

The [2005–06 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_Real_Madrid_CF_season) began with the promise of several new signings: [Júlio Baptista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%BAlio_Baptista) (€24 million), [Robinho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robinho) (€30 million), and [Sergio Ramos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Ramos) (€27 million).[^cite_note-85] However, Real Madrid suffered from some poor results, including a 0–3 loss at the hands of Barcelona at the Santiago Bernabéu in November 2005.[^cite_note-86] Madrid's coach [Wanderley Luxemburgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanderlei_Luxemburgo) was sacked the following month and his replacement was [Juan Ramón López Caro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Ram%C3%B3n_L%C3%B3pez_Caro).[^cite_note-87] A brief return to form came to an abrupt halt after Madrid lost the first leg of the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_Copa_del_Rey) semi-finals 6–1 to Real Zaragoza,[^cite_note-88] a defeat that was nearly reversed with a 4–0 home victory. Shortly after, Real Madrid were eliminated from the Champions League for a fourth successive year, this time at the hands of [Arsenal](2174.md). On 27 February 2006, Florentino Pérez resigned.[^cite_note-BBCPerez2006-89]

### Ramón Calderón era (2006–2009)

[Ramón Calderón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Calder%C3%B3n) was elected as club president on 2 July 2006 and subsequently appointed Fabio Capello as the new coach and [Predrag Mijatović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predrag_Mijatovi%C4%87) as the new sporting director. Real Madrid won the Liga title in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%E2%80%9307_La_Liga) for the first time in four years, but Capello was nonetheless sacked at the end of the campaign.[^cite_note-90] The title was won on 17 June, where Real faced [Mallorca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCD_Mallorca) at the Bernabéu while Barcelona and [Sevilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sevilla_FC), the other title challengers, faced [Gimnàstic de Tarragona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gimn%C3%A0stic_de_Tarragona) and [Villarreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villarreal_CF), respectively. At half-time, Real were 0–1 down, while Barcelona had surged ahead into a 0–3 lead in [Tarragona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nou_Estadi_de_Tarragona). However, three goals in the last half-hour secured Madrid a 3–1 win and their first league title since 2003.[^cite_note-Beckham-91] Real Madrid repeated as league winners in [2007–08](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007%E2%80%9308_La_Liga), but the [following season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_Real_Madrid_CF_season) ended up being one of the most disastrous in the club's history: Real was knocked out of the [Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_UEFA_Champions_League) at the round of 16 stage for the fifth time in a row, losing to [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.) 0–5 on aggregate, and was embarrassed by Barcelona at the Santiago Bernabéu with a humiliating 2–6 loss, which all but confirmed the [league title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_La_Liga) for Barça that went on to win the [treble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treble_\(association_football\)#Continental_trebles).[^cite_note-92]

### Second Florentino Pérez era (2009–present)



[Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md) was the club's most expensive signing when he joined in 2009, costing €94 million. He went on to become the club's all-time top scorer.

On 1 June 2009, Florentino Pérez regained Real Madrid's presidency amid the outrage over the club's decline.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] Pérez continued with the *Galácticos* policy pursued in his first term, buying [Kaká](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kak%C3%A1) from [Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.C._Milan) for a [record-breaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_association_football_transfers) (in pounds sterling) sum of £56 million,[^cite_note-95] and then breaking the record again by purchasing [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md) from [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.) for £80 million.[^cite_note-96] Real Madrid spent in excess of €261 million in the summer of 2009, and the assembled team was soon dubbed the *Second Galácticos*.[^cite_note-97] The [2009–10 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_Real_Madrid_CF_season), however, was a transitional one as Madrid again finished second in the [league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_La_Liga), although this time amassing 96 points, the club's record at the time, and went out of the [Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_UEFA_Champions_League) at the hands of [Lyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympique_Lyonnais). The season was marred by Cristiano Ronaldo's injury, that sidelined him for seven weeks,[^cite_note-98] although he still topped the goalscoring charts with 33 goals, and Madrid became the highest scoring team in La Liga, with 102 goals. Real Madrid also had the misfortune to become the runners-up with the highest points total in the history of Europe's top five leagues, until surpassed by Liverpool's 97 points in [2018–19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Premier_League).[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-Bleacher_Report-100]

[José Mourinho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mourinho) took over as manager in May 2010.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-102] In the [2010–11 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_Real_Madrid_CF_season), the rebuilt Madrid successfully fought on all fronts, going toe to toe with a brilliant [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_FC_Barcelona_season) side which some regard as the greatest team in football history. Ultimately, Madrid finished second in the [league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_La_Liga), with 92 points and four behind their perennial rivals, defeated them in the [Copa del Rey final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Copa_del_Rey_Final), and lost to Barça in the [Champions League semi-finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_UEFA_Champions_League_knockout_phase#Semi-finals), where Real progressed to for the first time since [2002–03](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_UEFA_Champions_League). Moreover, from 16 April through 3 May, a rare occurrence happened when, for the first time ever, four *[Clásicos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Cl%C3%A1sico)* were to be played in a span of just 18 days. The first fixture was in the league campaign on 16 April (which ended 1–1 with penalty goals for both sides), the second one was in the Copa del Rey final (which was won by Madrid 1–0 [a.e.t](633242.md#Association_football)., bringing them their first trophy in the second *Galáctico* era) on 20 April and the third and fourth ones in the controversial[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104] two-legged Champions League semi-finals on 27 April and 3 May (Barcelona won on aggregate with a 2–0 away victory and a 1–1 home draw).[^cite_note-105] Madrid again became the highest scoring team in La Liga, with 102 goals, repeating its output from the previous season, with Ronaldo scoring 40 and winning the [European Golden Shoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Golden_Shoe).

In the [2011–12 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Real_Madrid_CF_season), Real Madrid won [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_La_Liga) for a record 32nd time in its history, also finishing the season with numerous [league records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga_records_and_statistics) set, including 100 points amassed in a single season, a total of 121 goals scored, a goal difference of +89, 16 away games won, and 32 wins overall.[^cite_note-106] They also competed in the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_UEFA_Champions_League) for the 15th successive season,[^cite_note-107] losing in the semi-finals to [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich) in a [penalty shoot-out](511302.md) after a 3–3 aggregate tie. Madrid entered the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Copa_del_Rey) as the [defending champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_Copa_del_Rey), but lost 3–4 on aggregate in the quarter-finals to Barcelona. In the same season, Cristiano Ronaldo became the fastest player to reach 100 goals scored in Spanish league history. In reaching 101 goals in 92 games, Ronaldo surpassed Real Madrid legend [Ferenc Puskás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferenc_Pusk%C3%A1s), who scored 100 goals in 105 matches. Ronaldo set a new club mark for individual goals scored in one year (60) and became the first player ever to score against all 19 opposition teams in a single season.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109]

Real Madrid started the [2012–13 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_Real_Madrid_CF_season) by winning the [Supercopa de España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a), defeating Barcelona on away goals. However, the super cup turned out to be their only trophy of the season, despite being close to win them all. Real finished runners-up to Barça in [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_La_Liga), accumulating 85 points, and reached the semi-finals of the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_UEFA_Champions_League) for the third year in a row, where they were eliminated by [Borussia Dortmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borussia_Dortmund) 3–4 on aggregate. Madrid also entered the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_Copa_del_Rey) in the round of 32, going on a memorable run to the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Copa_del_Rey_Final), which saw them defeat Barcelona in the semi-finals before losing to [Atlético Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Madrid) 1–2 [a.e.t](633242.md#Association_football). Real Madrid faced the *Blaugrana* six times throughout the season, coming away with three wins, two draws, and one loss. A major transfer of the season was the signing of [Luka Modrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Modri%C4%87) from [Tottenham Hotspur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tottenham_Hotspur_F.C.) for a fee in the region of £33 million.[^cite_note-110] After a loss to Atlético in the Copa del Rey final, Pérez announced the departure of José Mourinho at the end of the season by "mutual agreement".[^cite_note-111]

#### *La Décima* and Champions League dominance

[**Di María**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81ngel_Di_Mar%C3%ADa)

[**Benzema**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Benzema)



Real Madrid players celebrate the club's historic tenth European Cup / Champions League win (*La Décima*) in 2014.

On 25 June 2013, [Carlo Ancelotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Ancelotti) succeeded Mourinho to become the manager of Real Madrid on a three-year deal, with Zinedine Zidane named as one of his assistants.[^cite_note-Ancelotti-112] On 1 September 2013, the long-awaited transfer of [Gareth Bale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gareth_Bale) from [Tottenham Hotspur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tottenham_Hotspur_F.C.) was announced. The transfer of the Welshman was reportedly a new world record signing, with the transfer price approximated at €100 million.[^cite_note-realmadrid2-113] In Ancelotti's [first season at the club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_Real_Madrid_CF_season), Real Madrid fought on all three fronts for the continental treble. Despite leading in the [league campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_La_Liga) on multiple occasions, Madrid ultimately finished in third place (level on points with [Barcelona](68187.md) and three behind cross-city rivals [Atlético Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Madrid)), collecting 87 points in total and scoring 104 goals.[^cite_note-114] By that time, *Los Blancos* had already secured the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_Copa_del_Rey) – against rivals Barcelona – in April, with Bale scoring the winner.[^cite_note-115] The major breakthrough came in the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_UEFA_Champions_League), where Real returned to the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_UEFA_Champions_League_Final) after twelve years, having beaten defending champions Bayern Munich 5–0 on aggregate in the semi-finals.[^cite_note-BBCBayern2014-116] In the final, they defeated then-recently-league winners Atlético Madrid 4–1 [a.e.t](633242.md#Association_football). to clinch their tenth European Cup (first since 2002) and become the first team to win ten European Cups/Champions League titles, an achievement known as "*La Décima*" (Spanish: "The Tenth", [\[la ˈðeθima\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Spanish)).[^cite_note-117] Real's attacking trio of Bale, Benzema and Cristiano, dubbed the BBC, finished the season with 97 goals.[^cite_note-118]

After winning the 2014 Champions League, Real Madrid signed goalkeeper [Keylor Navas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keylor_Navas), midfielder [Toni Kroos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toni_Kroos) and attacking midfielder [James Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Rodr%C3%ADguez).[^cite_note-119] In August, Madrid won the [2014 UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_UEFA_Super_Cup) against Sevilla, the club's 79th official trophy.[^cite_note-120] During the last week of the 2014 summer transfer window, Real Madrid sold two players key to the previous season's successes: [Xabi Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xabi_Alonso) to Bayern Munich and [Ángel Di María](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81ngel_Di_Mar%C3%ADa) to Manchester United. This decision by the club was surrounded by controversy, with Cristiano Ronaldo stating, "If I was in charge, maybe I would have done things differently", while Carlo Ancelotti admitted, "We must start again from zero."[^cite_note-121]

After a slow start to the [2014–15 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_Real_Madrid_CF_season), Real Madrid went on a record-breaking 22-match winning streak, which included wins against Barcelona and [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.), surpassing the previous Spanish record of 18 successive wins set by [Frank Rijkaard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Rijkaard)'s Barça in the [2005–06 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_FC_Barcelona_season).[^cite_note-122] In late December, Real Madrid won their first [Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_Club_World_Cup), defeating [San Lorenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Lorenzo_de_Almagro) 2–0 in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_Club_World_Cup_Final).[^cite_note-123] The winning streak came to an end in their opening match of 2015 with a loss to Valencia, leaving the club two short of equalling the world record of 24 consecutive wins.[^cite_note-124] Madrid was in contention for both the [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_La_Liga) title and the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_UEFA_Champions_League) until the end but ultimately came up short, finishing with 92 points in the league, two behind treble-winning Barcelona and losing to [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus_FC) 2–3 on aggregate in the Champions League semi-finals.[^cite_note-125] Ronaldo finished the season scoring 48 league goals, winning his fourth European Golden Shoe, and 61 goals in all competitions, breaking his record from 2011 to 2012.[^cite_note-126] Overall, despite playing an attractive attacking football and being the highest scoring team in Europe with 118 league goals,[^cite_note-127] several narrow defeats meant that Real finished the season with two trophies out of six possible, which contributed to the dismissal of Carlo Ancelotti on 25 May 2015.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-underthecoshblog.com-129]

On 3 June 2015, [Rafael Benítez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Ben%C3%ADtez) was confirmed as the new Real Madrid manager for the [2015–16 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_Real_Madrid_CF_season), signing a three-year contract.[^cite_note-130] Real Madrid remained unbeaten in the [league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_La_Liga) until a 3–2 loss at Sevilla on the matchday 11. This was followed by a 0–4 home loss in the first *Clásico* of the season against Barcelona. Perhaps, his reign is best remembered by multiple lopsided wins achieved both in La Liga and the [Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_UEFA_Champions_League) (6–0 vs [Espanyol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCD_Espanyol), 8–0 vs [Malmö](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malm%C3%B6_FF), 10–2 vs [Rayo Vallecano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayo_Vallecano) and others). In the [Copa del Rey round of 32](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_Copa_del_Rey#Round_of_32), Real accidentally fielded an ineligible player in a 3–1 first leg win at [Cádiz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A1diz_CF) and was disqualified from the tournament two days later by the competition judge, despite protests from president Pérez.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132][^cite_note-DQ-133] In the meantime, Madrid comfortably topped their [UCL group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_UEFA_Champions_League_group_stage#Group_A) with 16 points and a +16 goal difference. Benítez was relieved of his duties on 4 January 2016 following allegations of unpopularity with supporters, displeasure with players and a failure to produce good results against top teams.[^cite_note-134] Benítez's departure was announced along with the promotion of Zinedine Zidane to his first head coaching role.[^cite_note-Coach_Zidane-135] Under Zidane, Madrid managed to turn the odds in its favour, ultimately winning the Champions League, something no one expected. The notable results include a 2–1 away victory over reigning treble winners Barcelona, who were on a record-breaking winning streak, a fantastic comeback against [Wolfsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VfL_Wolfsburg) in the Champions League quarter-finals (after losing the away game 0–2, Madrid erased the deficit and won 3–0 at home, courtesy of a Cristiano Ronaldo hat-trick), as well as a 12-game winning streak to conclude the league campaign, meaning Real finished second, with 90 points and just one point behind champions Barcelona, coming agonizingly close to clinching the title and overcoming a 12-point deficit in the process.[^cite_note-136] Finally, on 28 May, Real Madrid's eleventh Champions League title was won thanks to a 5–3 penalty shoot-out victory over rivals Atlético Madrid after a 1–1 draw in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_UEFA_Champions_League_Final), with the achievement being termed "*La Undécima*".[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138][^cite_note-underthecoshblog.com-129]



2016–17 La Liga champions Real Madrid celebrate the title with Community of Madrid President [Cristina Cifuentes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristina_Cifuentes).

Real Madrid began their [2016–17 campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Real_Madrid_CF_season), which was to be Zidane's first full season in charge of the club, with a victory in the [2016 UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_UEFA_Super_Cup) against Sevilla.[^cite_note-139] On 10 December 2016, Madrid played their 35th-straight match without a loss, which set a new club record.[^cite_note-140] On 18 December 2016, the club defeated Japanese outfit [Kashima Antlers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashima_Antlers) 4–2 in the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_FIFA_Club_World_Cup_Final) of the [2016 FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_FIFA_Club_World_Cup).[^cite_note-141] With a 3–3 draw at Sevilla in the second leg of the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Copa_del_Rey) round of 16 on 12 January 2017, Madrid progressed to the quarter-finals with a 6–3 aggregate victory and extended its unbeaten run to 40 matches, breaking Barcelona's Spanish record of 39 matches unbeaten in all competitions from the [previous season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_FC_Barcelona_season).[^cite_note-142] Their unbeaten streak ended after a 1–2 away loss against the same opposition in [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_La_Liga) three days later.[^cite_note-143] The team then was knocked out of the Copa del Rey by [Celta Vigo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RC_Celta_de_Vigo) 3–4 on aggregate. These slips, however, did not affect the overall trajectory of the season. In May, Madrid won the league title for a record 33rd time, their first title in five years, accumulating 93 points in the process.[^cite_note-144] On 3 June 2017, the club's victory over [Juventus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juventus_FC) in the [Champions League final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_UEFA_Champions_League_Final) resulted in Real Madrid becoming the first team to successfully defend their title in the UEFA Champions League era, and the first to win consecutive titles in the competition since [Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.C._Milan) in [1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988%E2%80%9389_European_Cup) and [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989%E2%80%9390_European_Cup), when the tournament was known as the European Cup.[^cite_note-GuardianUCL2017-145][^cite_note-UEFACardiff2017-146] Real Madrid's title was its 12th, extending the record, and its third in four years. The achievement is also known as "La Duodécima".[^cite_note-147] The 2016–17 season was the greatest campaign in terms of trophies won (four out of possible five) in the history of Real Madrid, an achievement that would be later equalled in the [2017–18 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_Real_Madrid_CF_season).[^cite_note-148]

Real kicked off the 2017–18 campaign by winning its second consecutive and fourth overall [UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Super_Cup) in a [2–1 victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_UEFA_Super_Cup) against [Manchester United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_United_F.C.).[^cite_note-149] Five days later, Real Madrid beat Barcelona at the [Camp Nou](222488.md) 3–1 in the first leg of the [2017 Supercopa de España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a) and then defeated Barça 2–0 in the return leg, ending their 24 consecutive match scoring record in [*El Clásico* matches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_El_Cl%C3%A1sico_matches) and winning the second trophy of the season.[^cite_note-150] On 16 December 2017, Real beat Brazilian club [Grêmio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gr%C3%AAmio_Foot-Ball_Porto_Alegrense) 1–0 in the [FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_Club_World_Cup) [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_FIFA_Club_World_Cup_Final) and became the first team to retain the trophy.[^cite_note-151] In the [2017–18 UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_UEFA_Champions_League), Madrid once again progressed to the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_UEFA_Champions_League_Final) where they defeated Liverpool 3–1 to become the first club to win three straight titles in the Champions League era, as well as the first team to win three consecutive titles in the European Cup/Champions League since Bayern Munich in [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975%E2%80%9376_European_Cup). The trophy also marked Madrid's fourth win in five years and their eighth consecutive semi-finals appearance. On 31 May, only five days after winning the final, Zidane announced his resignation as Real Madrid manager, citing the club's "need for change" as his rationale for departing.[^cite_note-152][^cite_note-153] Zidane's and Ronaldo's departures marked the end of the *Second Galáctico Era* that yielded four Champions League titles, two La Liga titles, two Copa del Rey, two Supercopa de España, three UEFA Super Cups, and three FIFA Club World Cup titles. The team was instrumental in ending Barcelona's dominance, despite the *Blaugrana* boasting arguably the greatest collection of talent in history,[^cite_note-154] and overshadowed the Catalans on the European stage.[^cite_note-155] Real Madrid was also somewhat notoriously unlucky in its league campaigns throughout these nine years, finishing runners-up with 96, 92 (twice) and 90 points, as well as on 87 points in third place, just three off the league winners.[^cite_note-Bleacher_Report-100]

#### Following Ronaldo's departure



Chart of Real Madrid's league performance 1929–2025

On 12 June 2018, Real Madrid named [Julen Lopetegui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julen_Lopetegui), the head coach of the [Spanish national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain_men's_national_football_team), as their new manager. It was announced that he would officially begin his managerial duties after the [2018 FIFA World Cup](13327177.md). However, the Spanish national team sacked Lopetegui a day prior to the tournament, stating that he had negotiated terms with the club without informing them.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] The club then began re-shaping the squad in the summer of 2018, which included the sale of Cristiano Ronaldo to Juventus for a reported €117 million.[^cite_note-158] Madrid began their [2018–19 campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Real_Madrid_CF_season) by losing to Atlético Madrid 2–4 [a.e.t](633242.md#Association_football). in the [2018 UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_UEFA_Super_Cup). After a 1–5 loss to Barcelona in *El Clásico* on 28 October which left Real Madrid in the ninth place with only 14 points after ten games, Lopetegui was dismissed a day later and replaced by then [Castilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid_Castilla) coach, [Santiago Solari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Solari).[^cite_note-159] On 22 December 2018, Real Madrid beat [Al Ain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Ain_FC) 4–1 in the [FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_Club_World_Cup) and became the outright record winners of the Club World Cup with four titles.[^cite_note-Real_becomes_highest_trophy_holder_in_FIFA_Club_World_Cup-160] However, they were then knocked out of the [Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Copa_del_Rey) at the semi-final stage by Barcelona, losing 1–4 on aggregate. On 5 March 2019, Real was defeated by [Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Ajax) 1–4 (3–5 on aggregate) at home, crashing out of the [Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_UEFA_Champions_League) at the round of 16 stage after eight consecutive semi-finals appearances. On 11 March 2019, Real Madrid dismissed Solari and reinstated Zidane as the head coach of the club.[^cite_note-161] Madrid finished the season with 68 points and a +17 goal difference, its lowest totals since [2001–02](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001%E2%80%9302_La_Liga) and [1999–2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999%E2%80%932000_La_Liga) respectively.[^cite_note-162]

In the summer of 2019, Madrid signed [Eden Hazard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eden_Hazard), [Luka Jović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Jovi%C4%87), [Éder Militão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89der_Milit%C3%A3o), [Ferland Mendy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferland_Mendy), [Rodrygo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodrygo), [Reinier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinier_Jesus_Carvalho) and other players for a total of more than €350 million.[^cite_note-163] On 12 January 2020, Madrid beat cross-city rivals Atlético Madrid in a penalty shootout in the [Supercopa de España final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a_Final) to win their eleventh title.[^cite_note-164] After a three-month hiatus due to the [COVID-19 outbreak](63217011.md) in March 2020, [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_La_Liga) was restarted in June and Madrid won ten games in a row to capture the team's 34th league title, collecting 87 points in total.[^cite_note-165] From the competition's resumption in June and until the end of the [2020–21 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Real_Madrid_CF_season), Real temporarily played home fixtures at the [Alfredo Di Stéfano Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Di_St%C3%A9fano_Stadium), while the Santiago Bernabéu underwent extensive renovations.[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-Evening_Standard-167]

#### Further domestic success and *La Decimocuarta*



Real Madrid players celebrating the [double](88184.md#European_double) on 29 May 2022, the day after their Champions League victory



**[Courtois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thibaut_Courtois)**

**[Militão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89der_Milit%C3%A3o)**

**[Alaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Alaba)**

**[Carvajal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Carvajal)**

**[Mendy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferland_Mendy)**

**[Casemiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casemiro)**

**[Modrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luka_Modri%C4%87)**

**[Kroos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toni_Kroos)**

**[Valverde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Valverde)**

**[Vinícius Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vin%C3%ADcius_J%C3%BAnior)**

**[Benzema (C)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Benzema)**

Zidane left a second time on 27 May 2021 after going trophyless [that season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Real_Madrid_CF_season),[^cite_note-168] with Carlo Ancelotti returning to coach the team for the [2021–22 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_Real_Madrid_CF_season).[^cite_note-169] On the domestic front, he delivered two trophies out of possible three, winning [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_La_Liga) and the [Supercopa de España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a).[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171][^cite_note-BBCEspanyol2022-172] As such, Ancelotti won all six available top trophies at Madrid.[^cite_note-BBCEspanyol2022-172] In the [Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_UEFA_Champions_League), Madrid produced one of the most memorable runs of all time,[^cite_note-173] defeating pre-tournament favourites [Paris Saint-Germain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Saint-Germain_F.C.),[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] [defending champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_UEFA_Champions_League) and favourites [Chelsea](7473.md),[^cite_note-176] [Premier League champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Premier_League) and heavy favourites [Manchester City](165813.md),[^cite_note-177] all in dramatic fashion,[^cite_note-178] and setting up the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_UEFA_Champions_League_Final) against Liverpool, who were once again widely considered as favourites,[^cite_note-179] in a rematch of their 2018 encounter. A lone [Vinícius Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vin%C3%ADcius_J%C3%BAnior) goal sealed the 14th European Cup for *Los Blancos*, their fifth in nine years, and Ancelotti's second in charge.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181] Real also claimed their fourth ever [European double](88184.md#European_double) (after [1956–57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1956%E2%80%9357_Real_Madrid_CF_season), [1957–58](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1957%E2%80%9358_Real_Madrid_CF_season), and [2016–17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Real_Madrid_CF_season)). Despite Real's status as the most decorated team in the history of the Champions League, they were not favoured to win that year's edition, and their improbable run was widely considered a surprise.[^cite_note-182]

#### Even more success and *La Decimoquinta*

After a highly successful 2021–22 season, Real Madrid signed German defender [Antonio Rüdiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_R%C3%BCdiger) and French midfielder [Aurélien Tchouaméni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aur%C3%A9lien_Tchouam%C3%A9ni), while club legends Marcelo, Isco, Gareth Bale and Casemiro left the club. Real Madrid's [2022–23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%9323_Real_Madrid_CF_season) season began with lifting the [UEFA Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Super_Cup) and later the [FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Club_World_Cup). The season ended with Real Madrid winning the [2022–23 Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%9323_Copa_del_Rey), but losing the La Liga and Spanish Super Cup to [Barcelona](68187.md) and the Champions League to Manchester City, being defeated 5–1 on aggregate.[^cite_note-183]

In 2023, Real Madrid signed [Jude Bellingham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jude_Bellingham), who was given the number #5 jersey, previously used by Zinedine Zidane. Real also signed [Arda Güler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arda_G%C3%BCler) and [Fran García](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran_Garc%C3%ADa_\(footballer,_born_1999\)). Meanwhile, Vinícius Júnior was given the number #7 jersey, previously used by Raúl and Cristiano Ronaldo; while Rodrygo was given the number #11 jersey, previously used by Gareth Bale. [Karim Benzema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Benzema) left the club, while [Toni Kroos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toni_Kroos) would later announce that he would retire from football after the season.[^cite_note-184]

The [2023–24 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_Real_Madrid_CF_season) would prove to be another highly successful season for Los Blancos. First, Real Madrid lifted the Spanish Super Cup against Barcelona 4–1, taking revenge of the previous year's 3–0 defeat. Then, Real Madrid lifted the La Liga with relative ease, reaching 95 points, the second-best winning campaign by Real Madrid in La Liga history after the [2011–12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Real_Madrid_CF_season) 100 points season. In the [2023–24 UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_UEFA_Champions_League), Real Madrid knocked out [RB Leipzig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RB_Leipzig) in the Round of 16, then defending champions [Manchester City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_City) in penalties.[^cite_note-185]

In the semifinals, Real Madrid faced [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayern_Munich) again. The first match was a 2–2 draw, while the second match in the Bernabéu was marked by a double by [Joselu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joselu) in the last few minutes of the game, with Bayern Munich suffering a historic comeback after leading 1–0. Real Madrid faced [Borussia Dortmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borussia_Dortmund) in the final, and won 2–0, with [Dani Carvajal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Carvajal) being the man of the match and scoring with a header after a Toni Kroos corner, and Vinícius Júnior once again scoring in a UCL final. Real Madrid won their 15th Champions League without losing a single match during the campaign.[^cite_note-186]

#### Mbappé era and decline

Following the conclusion of the 2023–24 season, Real Madrid announced that striker [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) would be joining the club on a free transfer from [Paris Saint-Germain](357488.md) in July 2024, concluding one of the most highly anticipated transfer sagas in modern history.[^cite_note-187] On 18 December 2024, Real Madrid clinched their second trophy of the season, uplifting the inaugural [2024 FIFA Intercontinental Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_FIFA_Intercontinental_Cup) by beating [Pachuca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C.F._Pachuca) 3–0.[^cite_note-188]

Despite high expectations, Madrid endured a disappointing 2024–25 season, finishing as runners-up in all 3 domestic competitions to [Barcelona](68187.md). They suffered a 5–2 defeat to Barça in the [2025 Supercopa de España final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a_final) and were edged out 3–2 after extra time in the [2024-25 Copa del Rey final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Copa_del_Rey_final). The season also saw a humbling [Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_UEFA_Champions_League) quarter-final exit, as Real Madrid were eliminated by [Arsenal](2174.md) 5–1 on aggregate.[^cite_note-189]

Following these setbacks, Real Madrid announced that Carlo Ancelotti would depart as manager at the end of the season. Across his two spells as a manager, he won 15 titles, making him the most successful manager in the club's history. To improve the squad, Real Madrid secured two significant signings ahead of the 2025 [FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Club_World_Cup): English full-back [Trent Alexander-Arnold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trent_Alexander-Arnold) from [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.)[^cite_note-190] and Spanish centre-back [Dean Huijsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dean_Huijsen) from [Bournemouth](451121.md).[^cite_note-191]

On 25 May 2025, former player and [Bayer Leverkusen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayer_04_Leverkusen) manager [Xabi Alonso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xabi_Alonso) was appointed as Real Madrid manager on a three-year contract. The [2025 FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_FIFA_Club_World_Cup) marked his professional managerial debut, with Real Madrid drawing 1–1 against Al-Hilal before suffering a 4–0 defeat to Paris Saint-Germain in the semi-final. After the Club World Cup, Madrid finalized the signings of two other players: [Álvaro Carreras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_Carreras) from [Benfica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S.L._Benfica) and [Franco Mastantuono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco_Mastantuono) from [River Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Atl%C3%A9tico_River_Plate), registering the latter as a [Castilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid_Castilla) player.[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193] On 12 January 2026, Alonso and Real Madrid mutually parted ways one day after a 3–2 loss to Barcelona in the [Supercopa de España final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a_final).[^cite_note-194] Former player and Castilla manager Álvaro Arbeloa was officially announced as head coach on 13 January 2026, just one day after the Supercopa loss. His first match in charge was a 3–2 loss to [Albacete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albacete_Balompi%C3%A9) in the Copa del Rey on 14 January, which resulted in an early exit from the domestic cup. Real Madrid eliminated Manchester City in a 5–1 aggregate victory in the [2025–26 UEFA Champions League](74127997.md) Round of 16, but later got eliminated by Bayern Munich 6–4 on aggregate. Under Arbeloa, the team experienced a mixed run in La Liga, widening the points gap between themselves and first-place Barcelona.

On 7 May 2026, just three days before the El Clásico, a fight broke out between Valverde and his teammate, Aurélien Tchouaméni. Following the fight, Valverde required stitches because he suffered a cut that needed to be addressed medically.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] Valverde was found unconscious on the floor with blood all over him and suffering from partial amnesia after hitting his head.[^cite_note-RMOfficial2026-197] Subsequent to the altercation, Real Madrid initiated a disciplinary investigation. The process culminated in both players extending mutual apologies to each other. As a result, the club imposed a fine of €500 thousand on each player and formally concluded the internal inquiry.[^cite_note-RMOfficial2026-197]

On 10 May 2026, Real Madrid suffered a decisive 2–0 defeat that officially saw Barcelona secure the La Liga title for the 29th time at the newly refurbished Spotify Camp Nou.[^cite_note-198]

## Crests and colours

### Crests

- 1902

  1902

- 1908

  1908

- 1920

- 1931

  1931

- 2001

  2001

The first crest had a simple design consisting of a decorative interlacing of the three initials of the club, "MCF" for Madrid Club de Fútbol, in dark blue on a white shirt. The first change in the crest occurred in 1908 when the letters adopted a more streamlined form and appeared inside a circle.[^cite_note-Escudo_Real_Madrid-199] The next change in the configuration of the crest did not occur until the presidency of [Pedro Parages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Parages) in 1920. At that time, [King Alfonso XIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfonso_XIII) granted the club his royal patronage which came in the form of the title "Real Madrid", meaning "Royal".[^cite_note-200] Thus, Alfonso's crown was added to the crest and the club styled itself *Real Madrid Club de Fútbol*.[^cite_note-Escudo_Real_Madrid-199]

With the dissolution of the monarchy in 1931, all royal symbols (the crown on the crest and the title of Real) were eliminated. The crown was replaced by the dark mulberry band of the Region of Castile.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(IV)-52] In 1941, two years after [Nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_faction_\(Spanish_Civil_War\)) victory in the Civil War, the [Francoist regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francoist_Spain) restored the crest's "Real Corona", or "Royal Crown", while the mulberry stripe of [Castile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castile_\(historical_region\)) was retained as well.[^cite_note-goal.com-201][^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(V)-61] In addition, the whole crest was made full color, with gold being the most prominent, and the club went back to its honorific name *Real Madrid Club de Fútbol*.[^cite_note-Escudo_Real_Madrid-199] The most recent modification to the crest occurred in 2001 when the club wanted to better situate itself for the 21st century and further standardize its crest. One of the modifications made was changing the mulberry stripe to a more bluish shade.[^cite_note-Escudo_Real_Madrid-199]

### Colours

Wikimedia Commons logo

Wikimedia Commons has media related to [Real Madrid CF kits (1902–2000)](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Real%20Madrid%20CF%20kits%20\(1902%E2%80%932000\)).

Wikimedia Commons logo

Wikimedia Commons has media related to [Real Madrid CF kits (2000–present)](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Real%20Madrid%20CF%20kits%20\(2000%E2%80%93present\)).



Real Madrid in 1964. Nicknamed *Los Blancos* (The Whites), the club has worn an all-white home kit except for one season in 1925.

Real Madrid has maintained the white shirt for its home kit throughout the history of the club. There was, however, one season in 1926 that the shirt and shorts were not both white. It was an initiative undertaken by [Juan Padrós](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Padr%C3%B3s) in honour of an English team [Corinthian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corinthian_F.C.), which he had met on one of his trips, one of the most famous teams at the time known for its elegance and sportsmanship.[^cite_note-sapiens.cat-47] It was decided that Real Madrid would wear black shorts in an attempt to replicate the English team, which had also inspired Madrid's original white kit, but the initiative lasted just one year. After being eliminated from the [1926 Copa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1926_Copa_del_Rey) by Barcelona with a 1–5 defeat in Madrid and a 2–0 defeat in Catalonia, President Parages decided to return to an all-white kit, claiming that the other kit brought bad luck.[^cite_note-Origin-202] By the early 1940s, the manager changed the kit again by adding buttons to the shirt and the club's crest on the left breast, which has remained ever since. On 23 November 1947, in a game against [Atlético Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Madrid) at the Metropolitano Stadium, Real Madrid became the first Spanish team to wear numbered shirts.[^cite_note-Real_Madrid_turns_106_(V)-61] English club [Leeds United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leeds_United_F.C.) permanently switched their blue shirt for a white one in the 1960s, to emulate the dominant Real Madrid of the era.[^cite_note-203]

Real's traditional away colours are all blue or all purple. Since the advent of the replica kit market, the club has also released various other one colour designs, including red, green, orange and black. The club's kit is manufactured by [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas), whose contract extends from 1998.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] Real Madrid's first shirt sponsor, [Zanussi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zanussi), agreed for the 1982–83, 1983–84 and 1984–85 seasons. Following that, the club was sponsored by [Parmalat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parmalat) and Otaysa before a long-term deal was signed with [Teka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teka) in 1992.[^cite_note-Evolución_Del_Uniforme_del_Real_Madrid_(1902–1989)-206][^cite_note-Evolución_Del_Uniforme_del_Real_Madrid_(1991–2008)-207] In 2001, Real Madrid ended their contract with Teka and for one season and used the Realmadrid.com logo to promote the club's website. Then, in 2002, a deal was signed with [Siemens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siemens) and in 2006, the [BenQ Siemens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BenQ_Siemens) logo appeared on the club's shirt.[^cite_note-208] Real Madrid's shirt sponsor from 2007 until 2013 was [bwin.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bwin.com) following the economic problems of BenQ Siemens.[^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210] Fly Emirates became their shirt sponsor in 2013, and in 2017 the club renewed the sponsorship with the airliner, signing a deal until 2022 worth €70 million per year.[^cite_note-211] In 2022, the agreement was again extended, until 2026.[^cite_note-212] In 2015, Madrid signed a new ten-year contract with Adidas, believed to be worth a total of £850 million (€1 billion), with the club earning £59 million (€64 million) per season. The contract includes a clause sanctioning penalty or agreement termination anytime, if Real Madrid fails to qualify for the European competitions or is relegated from [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga).[^cite_note-213]
